● I've written the skill for you to review. Nothing in your project was changed,
  and I haven't tested it yet.

  Files
  - SKILL.md (.claude/skills/task-mgmt-api/SKILL.md) — the main instructions,
    about 300 lines.
  - references/code-templates.md
    (.claude/skills/task-mgmt-api/references/code-templates.md) — ready-to-copy
    code. Claude only reads it when it needs to write code.

  I put it at .claude/skills/task-mgmt-api/ because Claude Code expects a skill
  to be a folder containing a file named SKILL.md, not a standalone
  task-mgmt-api.md. Keeping it inside the repo means everyone working on the
  project gets it.

  What the skill covers
  - Structure: four projects (Domain, Application, Infrastructure, Api) plus
    test projects. One rule is enforced by tests: the business logic layer never
    references EF Core or ASP.NET, and controllers never use the database
    directly.
  - Domain: entities whose rules live inside them, with private setters and
    methods like Complete(). IDs have their own types, and changes raise events.
    The example entity is TaskItem rather than Task, because Task and
    TaskStatus clash with .NET's built-in System.Threading.Tasks types.
  - Application: one folder per use case, with a command or query, a handler and
    a validator. Errors come back as a result object instead of exceptions.
    Data is returned as plain DTOs, never as the entities themselves.
  - EF Core code-first with SQL Server: all mapping is configured in code,
    strings get length limits, and there's a column for detecting conflicting
    edits. It includes the exact dotnet ef migration commands and a rule to
    review each migration before applying it. Migrations run automatically only
    in Development.
  - REST: routes like api/v1/tasks, standard status codes, actions such as POST
    /tasks/{id}/complete, paginated lists, and errors in the standard
    ProblemDetails format with a global exception handler.
  - Workflows: steps for turning your current template into this structure,
    adding a new feature layer by layer, and changing an existing entity.
  - Scaling: notes on splitting into separate modules, faster read queries, the
    outbox pattern, and adding login/auth.

  Decisions I'd like you to check
  1. Project names: I used TaskManagement.Domain, TaskManagement.Api and so on,
     instead of the template's Task_Management_System.
  2. No MediatR or AutoMapper: both now need a paid license, so the skill uses
     simple handler interfaces and mapping written by hand. FluentValidation
     (free) handles validation.
  3. Guid IDs generated in code, and enums stored in the database as text.
  4. Your existing template project: the skill tells Claude to ask you before
     moving or deleting it.

  Worth knowing: Microsoft ends support for .NET 8 on November 10, 2026, about a
  month from now. If this API is going to run in production for a long time,
  consider .NET 10 (the current long-term release) instead. If you'd like that,
  I'll update the skill.

  Once you've reviewed it, tell me what to change. After that I can run 2–3
  realistic test prompts with and without the skill, such as "set up the
  solution" or "add Projects and let tasks belong to a project", so you can
  compare the results.

> We need to use .net 8 only. make the changes in skills file and implement the
  changes with the skill file

      27 +**This project targets .NET 8 only. That is a fixed project dec
         +ision.** Every project uses `net8.0`. Every `Microsoft.*` / EF 
         +Core package uses an **8.0.x** version, and the `dotnet-ef` too
         +l is 8.0.x. Don't upgrade to .NET 9/10 or EF Core 9/10, and don
         +'t accept a package version that needs a newer framework, even 
         +if a newer SDK is installed or a tool suggests it. When adding 
         +a package with `dotnet add package`, always pass `--version` ex
         +plicitly. Otherwise NuGet picks the latest major version, which
         + targets a newer framework and fails restore or silently drifts
         +. Third-party packages (FluentValidation, Swashbuckle, NetArchT
         +est, xUnit) must use versions that support `net8.0`.

● All endpoints work as expected, but I found a bug: createdAtUtc loses its
  trailing Z because SQL Server's datetime2 doesn't preserve DateTime kind,
  causing EF to read it as "Unspecified." I'll fix this by adding a UTC
  convention in the DbContext.

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 15 lines
      26  
      27  **This project targets .NET 8 only. That is a fixed project dec
          ision.** Every project uses `net8.0`. Every `Microsoft.*` / EF 
          Core package uses an **8.0.x** version, and the `dotnet-ef` too
          l is 8.0.x. Don't upgrade to .NET 9/10 or EF Core 9/10, and don
          't accept a package version that needs a newer framework, even 
          if a newer SDK is installed or a tool suggests it. When adding 
          a package with `dotnet add package`, always pass `--version` ex
          plicitly. Otherwise NuGet picks the latest major version, which
           targets a newer framework and fails restore or silently drifts
          . Third-party packages (FluentValidation, Swashbuckle, NetArchT
          est, xUnit) must use versions that support `net8.0`.
      28  
      29 +Pinned versions currently in use (check the csproj files before
         + adding a package, and match them):                            
      30 +                                                               
      31 +| Package | Version | Project |                                
      32 +|---|---|---|                                                  
      33 +| Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.SqlServer | 8.0.31 | Infrastruc
         +ture |                                                         
      34 +| Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design | 8.0.31 | Api |        
      35 +| Microsoft.Extensions.Configuration.Abstractions | 8.0.0 | Inf
         +rastructure |                                                  
      36 +| Microsoft.Extensions.DependencyInjection.Abstractions | 8.0.2
         + | Application |                                               
      37 +| FluentValidation (+ .DependencyInjectionExtensions) | 12.2.0 
         +| Application |                                                
      38 +| Swashbuckle.AspNetCore | 6.6.2 | Api |                       
      39 +| NetArchTest.Rules | 1.3.2 | ArchitectureTests |              
      40 +| dotnet-ef (local tool, `.config/dotnet-tools.json`) | 8.0.31 
         +| repo |                                                       
      41 +                                                               
      42 +To find the newest 8.0.x patch of a Microsoft package, query `h
         +ttps://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/<id-lowercase>/index.json
         +` and filter for `^8\.0\.\d+$`. When bumping a patch, bump all 
         +EF Core packages and the `dotnet-ef` tool together.

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      144  - `AppDbContext : DbContext, IUnitOfWork` exposes `DbSet<TaskI
           tem>` and calls `modelBuilder.ApplyConfigurationsFromAssembly(
           ...)`.
      145  - **All mapping is Fluent API** in `Persistence/Configurations
           /<Entity>Configuration.cs` (`IEntityTypeConfiguration<T>`). Do
           n't use data annotations, since they would leak EF into the do
           main.
      146  - In every configuration: set the table name, key, the strongl
           y-typed ID conversion, `HasMaxLength` on every string (otherwi
           se SQL Server gets `nvarchar(max)`), `IsRequired` where needed
           , enum conversions (`HasConversion<string>()` with max length 
           so the DB stays readable), and indexes for columns you filter 
           on.
      147 +- **UTC round-tripping:** SQL Server `datetime2` does not stor
          +e `DateTimeKind`, so EF reads values back as `Unspecified` and
          + the JSON loses its `Z` suffix (clients then treat it as local
          + time). `AppDbContext.ConfigureConventions` registers `UtcDate
          +TimeConverter` for all `DateTime`/`DateTime?` properties. Keep
          + it, and don't add per-property date conversions.             
      148  - Add a `rowversion` concurrency token (`byte[] RowVersion`, `
           .IsRowVersion()`) to aggregates that multiple users can edit c
           oncurrently.
      149  - Repositories implement the domain interfaces and expose only
            what the aggregate needs (`GetByIdAsync`, `Add`, `Remove`). D
           on't build a generic `IRepository<T>` that exposes `IQueryable
           `, because that lets query logic leak everywhere.
      150  - Register with `services.AddDbContext<AppDbContext>(o => o.Us
           eSqlServer(connectionString, sql => sql.EnableRetryOnFailure()
           ))`.

      268  
      269  Before coding, decide whether the new concept is its **own agg
           regate** or **part of an existing one**. Rule of thumb: if it 
           has its own lifecycle and is edited independently (a Project),
            it's an aggregate referenced **by ID** (`ProjectId` on `TaskI
           tem`, not a `Project` navigation property). If it only exists 
           inside its parent and must stay consistent with it (checklist 
           items in a task), it's a child entity in the parent aggregate.
            Referencing other aggregates by ID keeps aggregates small and
            makes splitting into separate services possible later.

      502  ```csharp
      503 +// Persistence/Converters/UtcDateTimeConverter.cs             
      504 +using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Storage.ValueConversion;  
      505 +                                                              
      506 +namespace TaskManagement.Infrastructure.Persistence.Converters
          +;                                                             
      507 +                                                              
      508 +/// SQL Server datetime2 does not store DateTimeKind, so value
          +s come back as Unspecified.                                   
      509 +/// All times in this system are UTC; mark them as such on rea
          +d so JSON serializes them with "Z".                           
      510 +internal sealed class UtcDateTimeConverter() : ValueConverter<
          +DateTime, DateTime>(                                          
      511 +    v => v.Kind == DateTimeKind.Local ? v.ToUniversalTime() : 
          +v,                                                            
      512 +    v => DateTime.SpecifyKind(v, DateTimeKind.Utc));          
      513 +```

● The skill is now locked to .NET 8 and the solution is built from it. The build
  has no warnings or errors, all 14 tests pass, the database is created in
  LocalDB, and every endpoint returned the status codes I expected.

  Skill changes (SKILL.md (.claude/skills/task-mgmt-api/SKILL.md),
  code-templates.md (.claude/skills/task-mgmt-api/references/code-templates.md))
  - .NET 8 only, every time: all projects target net8.0, all EF Core and
    Microsoft.* packages stay on 8.0.x, and upgrades are not allowed. This rule
    matters in practice. When I checked NuGet, a plain dotnet add package would
    have installed EF Core 10.0.12.
  - Pinned versions: the skill lists the exact version of every package (EF Core
    8.0.31, FluentValidation 12.2.0, and so on), with instructions for finding
    newer 8.0.x patches.
  - dotnet-ef 8.0.31 pinned inside the repo in .config/dotnet-tools.json. Your
    global dotnet-ef is 9.0.5; I left it alone, and inside this repo the pinned
    8.0.31 is used. After a fresh clone, run dotnet tool restore once.
  - UTC dates: a rule and template for the timestamp fix described below.

  What was built
  - TaskManagement.sln, with the SDK pinned to 8.0.425 in global.json.
  - Domain: the TaskItem entity with its rules (create, update, start, complete,
    reopen), its own ID type, and events.
  - Application: 8 use cases with validators, error results and paging.
  - Infrastructure: AppDbContext, the table mapping, a repository, a fast
    read-only query for listing, event dispatch, and the InitialCreate
    migration.
  - Api: TasksController.cs
    (src/TaskManagement.Api/Controllers/TasksController.cs) on api/v1/tasks,
    standard error responses, Swagger, and ready-to-run requests in
    TaskManagement.Api.http (src/TaskManagement.Api/TaskManagement.Api.http).
  - Tests: 9 for the domain rules, 2 for a handler, and 3 that fail if a layer
    references one it shouldn't.

  A bug the running API caught: timestamps came back without the Z that marks
  them as UTC, because SQL Server doesn't keep that information. Clients would
  have read them as local time. I added a UTC conversion for all date fields,
  confirmed they now end in Z, and confirmed it needed no database change.

  Your call: the old template folder Task_Management_System/ is untouched. It's
  no longer part of the solution, so it can be deleted. Want me to remove it?

  The database is TaskManagementDb on (localdb)\MSSQLLocalDB. To run the API,
  use dotnet run --project src/TaskManagement.Api and open /swagger.

● Written for: you and anyone else working in this repo, as a checklist to track
  the work against.

  I created task.md at the repo root. I haven't changed any code yet.

  What's in it
  - T1, Task entity: a checklist by layer for adding AssignedTo, CreatedDate,
    ModifiedDate and IsDeleted. Soft delete uses a global query filter so
    deleted tasks are hidden from every query. The migration is named
    AddAssignedToAndSoftDelete.
  - T2a–T2f, APIs: one section per endpoint from your list, each with its route,
    request fields, status codes and what counts as done. The summary endpoint
    has an example JSON response.
  - An endpoint overview table, and a backlog section at the bottom for new
    tasks.

  Requirements that don't match the current code (proposed answers are in the
  "Open decisions" table in the file):
  1. Routes: your spec says /api/tasks/summary, but the code uses /api/v1/tasks.
     I propose switching to /api/tasks.
  2. Status values: the spec wants To Do / In Progress / Done, and the code has
     Open / InProgress / Completed. The migration would convert existing rows
     rather than drop them.
  3. Update and status: I propose that PUT can change the status, and that the
     extra /start, /complete and /reopen endpoints are removed because they
     aren't in your requirements.
  4. Field names: CreatedAtUtc would be renamed to CreatedDate to match the
     spec.
  5. Summary contents: your spec doesn't say what the summary returns. I
     proposed total count, counts by status and by priority, and the number of
     unassigned tasks.

  Tell me which proposals to change, or confirm them, and I'll implement T1 and
  T2 and tick them off in the file as I go.

  3. remove extra end points

  5. Summary will be returned later. just create a api for it.

  make all the above mentioned changes in task and implement the changes

           h, not anemic:
      115  
      116 -- **Aggregates own their invariants.** Properties have `privat
          -e set`. State changes happen through intention-revealing metho
          -ds (`task.Complete(now)`, `task.Reschedule(dueDate)`), not by 
          -setting properties from outside. Creation goes through a stati
          -c factory (`TaskItem.Create(...)`) that validates inputs.

          +Update(...)`, `task.Delete(now)`), not by setting properties f
          +rom outside. Creation goes through a static factory (`TaskItem
          +.Create(...)`) that validates inputs.                         
      117  - **Throw `DomainException`** (or return a domain error) when 
           an invariant is violated, e.g. completing an already-completed
            task. Application/Api translate it to a 400/409 response.
      118  - **Strongly-typed IDs** (`readonly record struct TaskItemId(G
           uid Value)`) stop you from passing a `ProjectId` where a `Task
           ItemId` is expected. Generate them in the domain (`TaskItemId.
           New()`), not in the database.
      119  - **Value objects** (`ValueObject` base or `record`) for conce
           pts with no identity (e.g. `DateRange`, `Email`). Map them wit
           h EF owned types or `ComplexProperty`.

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      118  - **Strongly-typed IDs** (`readonly record struct TaskItemId(G
           uid Value)`) stop you from passing a `ProjectId` where a `Task
           ItemId` is expected. Generate them in the domain (`TaskItemId.
           New()`), not in the database.
      119  - **Value objects** (`ValueObject` base or `record`) for conce
           pts with no identity (e.g. `DateRange`, `Email`). Map them wit
           h EF owned types or `ComplexProperty`.
      120  - **Domain events** (`TaskItemCompleted`) are raised inside ag
           gregate methods and dispatched after `SaveChanges` succeeds. U
           se them for side effects (notifications, audit) so aggregates 
           don't depend on services.
      121 +- **Audit fields and soft delete (project standard):** aggrega
          +tes have `CreatedDate` (set in `Create`), `ModifiedDate` (set 
          +by every mutating method), `IsDeleted`, and `DeletedDate`. Row
          +s are never removed from the database. Deleting means calling 
          +`aggregate.Delete(nowUtc)`, and mutating methods call `EnsureN
          +otDeleted()` first. Use these field names (not `CreatedAtUtc`)
          + because they are part of the agreed spec; the values are stil
          +l UTC.                                                        
      122  - **Time is injected.** Pass `DateTime nowUtc` into domain met
           hods. Application gets it from .NET 8's built-in `TimeProvider
           `. Store all times in UTC.
      123  - **A private parameterless constructor** is allowed for EF Co
           re. It's the only concession the domain makes to persistence.
      124  - **Repository interfaces** (`ITaskItemRepository`) live next 
           to their aggregate in Domain, one per aggregate root only (nev
           er for child entities).

      149  - In every configuration: set the table name, key, the strongl
           y-typed ID conversion, `HasMaxLength` on every string (otherwi
           se SQL Server gets `nvarchar(max)`), `IsRequired` where needed
           , enum conversions (`HasConversion<string>()` with max length 
           so the DB stays readable), and indexes for columns you filter 
           on.
      150  - **UTC round-tripping:** SQL Server `datetime2` does not stor
           e `DateTimeKind`, so EF reads values back as `Unspecified` and
            the JSON loses its `Z` suffix (clients then treat it as local
            time). `AppDbContext.ConfigureConventions` registers `UtcDate
           TimeConverter` for all `DateTime`/`DateTime?` properties. Keep
            it, and don't add per-property date conversions.
      151  - Add a `rowversion` concurrency token (`byte[] RowVersion`, `
           .IsRowVersion()`) to aggregates that multiple users can edit c
           oncurrently.
      152 -- Repositories implement the domain interfaces and expose only
          - what the aggregate needs (`GetByIdAsync`, `Add`, `Remove`). D
          -on't build a generic `IRepository<T>` that exposes `IQueryable
          -`, because that lets query logic leak everywhere.             
      152 +- Repositories implement the domain interfaces and expose only
          + what the aggregate needs (`GetByIdAsync`, `Add`). There is no
          + `Remove`, because deletes are soft. Every soft-deletable enti
          +ty's configuration has `HasQueryFilter(x => !x.IsDeleted)`, so
          + deleted rows are invisible everywhere. Call `IgnoreQueryFilte
          +rs()` only on purpose, for audit or restore features. Don't bu
          +ild a generic `IRepository<T>` that exposes `IQueryable`, beca
          +use that lets query logic leak everywhere.                    
      153  - Register with `services.AddDbContext<AppDbContext>(o => o.Us

      154  - Domain events are dispatched from `AppDbContext.SaveChangesA

      197  ## 6. Api layer rules (RESTful design)
      198  
      199  - **Thin controllers**: map request → command/query, call hand
           ler, map `Result` → HTTP response. No business logic, no EF.
      200 -- Route convention: `api/v1/<plural-resource>` in kebab-case (
          -`api/v1/tasks`, `api/v1/tasks/{id:guid}`). The `v1` prefix mak
          -es versioning possible later without breaking clients.        
      201 -- HTTP verbs and status codes:                                
      200 +- Route convention (agreed spec): **`api/<plural-resource>`** 
          +in kebab-case, with **no version segment** (`api/tasks`, `api/
          +tasks/{id:guid}`). Don't reintroduce `api/v1`. If versioning i
          +s ever needed, discuss it with the user first.                
      201 +- Always constrain id parameters (`{id:guid}`) so literal sub-
          +routes like `summary` don't collide with them.                
      202 +- HTTP verbs and status codes (current Tasks API):            
      203  
      203 -| Action | Verb + route | Success |                           
      204 -|---|---|---|                                                 
      205 -| List (paged, filterable) | `GET /api/v1/tasks?page=1&pageSiz
          -e=20&status=Open` | 200 with paged envelope |                 
      206 -| Get one | `GET /api/v1/tasks/{id}` | 200 / 404 |            
      207 -| Create | `POST /api/v1/tasks` | 201 + `Location` header (`Cr
          -eatedAtAction`) |                                             
      208 -| Full update | `PUT /api/v1/tasks/{id}` | 204 |              
      209 -| State transition | `POST /api/v1/tasks/{id}/complete` | 204 
          -|                                                             
      210 -| Delete | `DELETE /api/v1/tasks/{id}` | 204 / 404 |          
      204 +| Action | Verb + route | Success | Errors |                  
      205 +|---|---|---|---|                                             
      206 +| List (paged; optional `status`, `priority`) | `GET /api/task
          +s?status=ToDo&priority=High&page=1&pageSize=20` | 200 with pag
          +ed envelope | 400 |                                           
      207 +| Summary | `GET /api/tasks/summary` | 200 | — |              
      208 +| Get one | `GET /api/tasks/{id}` | 200 | 404 (missing or dele
          +ted) |                                                        
      209 +| Create | `POST /api/tasks` | 201 + `Location` header (`Creat
          +edAtAction`) | 400 |                                          
      210 +| Full update (including `status`) | `PUT /api/tasks/{id}` | 2
          +04 | 400, 404, 409 |                                          
      211 +| Soft delete | `DELETE /api/tasks/{id}` | 204 | 404 |        
      212  
      212 -  Model domain behaviours (complete, assign, reopen) as sub-re
          -source actions rather than letting clients PATCH the `Status` 
          -field. This keeps the invariants in the domain.               
      213 +- **Status changes go through `PUT`** (agreed spec). Don't add
          + action endpoints like `/start`, `/complete`, `/reopen`. Statu
          +s rules belong in the domain's `Update` method.               
      214 +- `DELETE` is always a **soft delete**. Document that on the a
          +ction, and never hard-delete through the API.                 
      215 +- Only add endpoints that a work item in `task.md` asks for.  
      216  - **Errors use RFC 7807 ProblemDetails** everywhere: `builder.
           Services.AddProblemDetails()` plus a .NET 8 `IExceptionHandler
           ` (`GlobalExceptionHandler`) for unhandled exceptions, and a `
           ResultExtensions.ToProblem()` that maps `ErrorType` → status (
           Validation→400, NotFound→404, Conflict→409). Never return stac
           k traces to clients.
      217  - Request/response contracts live in `Api/Contracts` and are s
           eparate from Application commands, so the HTTP shape can evolv
           e independently.
      218  - Lists are always paginated (cap `pageSize`, e.g. at 100). Un
           bounded lists are a common scaling problem.

● I've made all five changes, and the API is working against your local SQL
  Server database. The build has no warnings or errors, all 22 tests pass, and I
  ran every endpoint against the real database.

  What was implemented
  1. Routes: everything is under /api/tasks. The old /api/v1/... routes now
     return 404.
  2. Status values: they are now To Do / In Progress / Done. I tested the
     migration on two rows in the old format: Open became To Do and Completed
     became Done.
  3. Extra endpoints removed: /start, /complete and /reopen are gone. Status is
     now changed through PUT /api/tasks/{id}.
  4. Field names: CreatedAtUtc was renamed to CreatedDate without losing data. I
     also added AssignedTo, ModifiedDate, IsDeleted and DeletedDate.
  5. Summary: GET /api/tasks/summary exists and returns {} for now. It's listed
     in task.md as backlog item T3 for when you define what it should contain.

  What the tests showed
  - List: filtering works by status, by priority, or both together. Asking for
    more than 100 per page returns 400.
  - Create: returns 201 with the new task's location, or 400 when the title is
    empty.
  - Update: returns 204 and sets ModifiedDate. An invalid status returns 400 and
    an unknown ID returns 404.
  - Soft delete: returns 204. The task then gets 404 on read and disappears from
    the list, but the row is still in the database with IsDeleted = 1.

  One behaviour change: before, a completed task couldn't be edited. Since
  status now changes through PUT, a task marked Done can be edited or moved back
  to In Progress. A deleted task can't be changed at all.

  Other updates
  - task.md now records your decisions, and the finished items are ticked.
  - The skill now treats /api/tasks without a version, soft delete only, status
    changes through PUT, and the new field names as project rules. It also tells
    Claude to work from task.md.
  - The code-templates file now points to the real Tasks code instead of keeping
    its own copy, so the two can't get out of sync.

  I removed the test rows I created during these checks from your local
  database.

      121 +| `"priority": 99` | 400 from FluentValidation | Works, but th

          +rser internals |                                              
      123 +| `?status=Bogus` | 400 `"The value 'Bogus' is not valid."` | 
          +Doesn't list the allowed values |

      153 +## T5: Global exception handling and consistent error response
          +s [ ]                                                         
      154 +                                                              
      155 +**Goal:** every error from every endpoint (validation, not fou
          +nd, domain rule, conflict, unknown route, server error) return
          +s **the same JSON format** with a correct HTTP status, a stabl
          +e machine-readable **error code**, and a human-readable messag
          +e.                                                            
      156 +                                                              
      157 +**Current state (checked 2026-10-11):** `GlobalExceptionHandle
          +r` (`IExceptionHandler`) and `ResultExtensions.ToProblem()` ex
          +ist, but:                                                     
      158 +- The error code is placed in `title` (e.g. `"Task.NotFound"`)
          +, and there's no dedicated `code` field.                      
      159 +- Validation errors come in two different formats (see T4).   
      160 +- An unknown route (404) and the wrong HTTP method (405) retur
          +n an **empty body**.                                          
      161 +- Not handled yet: `BadHttpRequestException` (e.g. body too la
          +rge), client-cancelled requests, and `DbUpdateException` (e.g.
          + constraint violations).                                      
      162 +                                                              
      163 +**Standard error format** (RFC 7807 ProblemDetails plus extens
          +ions):                                                        
      164 +```json                                                       
      165 +{                                                             
      166 +  "type": "https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc9110#section-15.5.5"
          +,                                                             
      167 +  "title": "Not Found",                                       
      168 +  "status": 404,                                              
      169 +  "code": "TASK_NOT_FOUND",                                   
      170 +  "detail": "Task '3fa85f64-...' was not found.",             
      171 +  "instance": "/api/tasks/3fa85f64-...",                      
      172 +  "traceId": "00-...",                                        
      173 +  "errors": { "title": ["Title is required."] }               
      174 +}                                                             
      175 +```                                                           
      176 +`errors` appears only for validation failures. `title` is the 
          +standard reason phrase for the status. `code` is what clients 
          +should switch on.                                             
      177 +                                                              
      178 +**Error codes** (proposal: decide in D7)                      
      179 +                                                              
      180 +| Code | HTTP | When |                                        
      181 +|---|---|---|                                                 
      182 +| `VALIDATION_FAILED` | 400 | FluentValidation or model-bindin
          +g failure (T4) |                                              
      183 +| `INVALID_REQUEST` | 400 | Malformed or unreadable request (`
          +BadHttpRequestException`) |                                   
      184 +| `DOMAIN_RULE_VIOLATED` | 400 | `DomainException` (e.g. modif
          +ying a deleted task) |                                        
      185 +| `TASK_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | Task missing or soft-deleted |     
      186 +| `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` | 404 | No endpoint matches the URL |     
      187 +| `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED` | 405 | Wrong HTTP verb for the route |
      188 +| `CONCURRENCY_CONFLICT` | 409 | `DbUpdateConcurrencyException
          +` (RowVersion) |                                              
      189 +| `DATABASE_ERROR` | 500 | `DbUpdateException` / SQL errors (d
          +etails logged, not returned) |                                
      190 +| `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR` | 500 | Any other unhandled exceptio
          +n |                                                           
      191 +                                                              
      192 +**Implementation**                                            
      193 +- [ ] Create an **`ErrorCode` enum** in `Application/Abstracti
          +ons` (values above), serialized as the upper-snake-case string
          +s. Replace the free-text codes in `Error` and `TaskErrors` wit
          +h it.                                                         
      194 +- [ ] Give `DomainException` an optional error code so specifi
          +c domain rules can have their own codes later.                
      195 +- [ ] One `ProblemDetailsFactory` / helper in `Api/Infrastruct
          +ure` that builds **every** error response, so `ResultExtension
          +s.ToProblem()`, `GlobalExceptionHandler`, and the model-state 
          +factory (T4) all produce the identical format.                
      196 +- [ ] `ResultExtensions.ToProblem()`: map `ErrorType` → status
          + and `ErrorCode` → `code`; put the message in `detail` and the
          + reason phrase in `title`.                                    
      197 +- [ ] `GlobalExceptionHandler`: map `DomainException` → 400, `
          +DbUpdateConcurrencyException` → 409, `BadHttpRequestException`
          + → 400 (using its status code), `DbUpdateException` → 500 `DAT
          +ABASE_ERROR`, everything else → 500 `INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR`. C
          +lient-cancelled requests (`OperationCanceledException` with `R
          +equestAborted`) are logged at Information and get no error bod
          +y.                                                            
      198 +- [ ] Logging: 5xx at **Error** with the exception, 4xx at **W
          +arning** without a stack trace. Include `traceId` and `code` i
          +n every log entry.                                            
      199 +- [ ] **Never** return exception messages, stack traces, or SQ
          +L details for 5xx. Use a generic message plus `traceId` so sup
          +port can find the log entry.                                  
      200 +- [ ] `app.UseStatusCodePages()` (with `AddProblemDetails`) so
          + 404 unknown route and 405 wrong method get the standard forma
          +t with `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` / `METHOD_NOT_ALLOWED`.              
      201 +- [ ] Add `instance` (request path) and `traceId` to every res
          +ponse via `ProblemDetailsOptions.CustomizeProblemDetails`.    
      202 +- [ ] Swagger: add `[ProducesResponseType<ProblemDetails>]` fo

      207 +- [ ] A test endpoint or stubbed handler that throws, to verif
          +y the 500 response hides details.                             
      208 +                                                              
      209 +**Acceptance criteria**                                       
      210 +- All error responses from all endpoints, including unknown ro
          +utes and 405, have the same format with a `code` from the tabl
          +e.                                                            
      211 +- 5xx responses never contain internal details, and the full e

● I've added the two work items to task.md. The file is task.md, not tasks.md,
  so I used the existing one. I haven't changed any code.

  Before writing them, I sent bad input to the running API to see what it
  returns today. That's what the items are built around.

  T4: Input validation and enums
  - Enums: the Status and Priority enums already exist. The task keeps them and
    makes JSON accept enum names only. Today "priority": 99 gets through to the
    validator as a number.
  - Rules: a full list per endpoint, such as Title required and Status/Priority
    must be valid. Error messages will list the allowed values.
  - Main problem found: invalid input currently comes back in three different
    formats depending on where it's caught. A bad enum string like "Urgent" also
    exposes an internal .NET type name in the response. The task puts all of
    these into one format with errors listed per field.

  T5: Global exception handling and consistent errors
  - One error format for every endpoint, with a separate code field, a message,
    traceId and the request path.
  - Error code table: 9 codes, such as VALIDATION_FAILED (400), TASK_NOT_FOUND
    (404), CONCURRENCY_CONFLICT (409) and INTERNAL_SERVER_ERROR (500), each with
    its HTTP status.
  - Gaps it closes: unknown routes (404) and wrong HTTP methods (405) currently
    return an empty body. Server errors must never expose internal details; the
    full error goes to the log under the same traceId.
  - Tests: a new integration test project with one test per error code.

  Four decisions in the file for you to confirm before I start:
  - D7, error code format: an ErrorCode enum shown as TASK_NOT_FOUND, instead of
    today's Task.NotFound strings.
  - D8, enum input: accept names only, not numbers.
  - D9, test database: integration tests use a separate local database rather
    than the real one.
  - D10, field names in errors: validation errors are keyed by the field names
    the client sends, e.g. title rather than Title.

  Confirm or change those, and I'll implement T4 and T5.

> For
  D7: Use format Task.NotFound
  D8: Is the validation part
  D9: Use the real database will run the initial integration after all errors
  are resolved
  D10: it should send the fields name as Title

● Here's how I'm reading your answers:

  - D7: error codes use dotted strings like Task.NotFound, defined as constants
    in one place. No enum.
  - D8: checking enum values is part of validation. A bad Status or Priority
    comes back as a normal validation error on that field, not as a JSON parsing
    error. To make that work, requests carry enum fields as text and the
    validators check them against the allowed names.
  - D9: integration tests run against the real database. I'll fix the error
    handling first and run them at the end. Most tests only read, or use stubs
    that don't touch the database.
  - D10: error keys use C# names such as Title.

  I'll start with the package version I need:

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Next, the Domain and Application pieces: error codes, field-level validation
  errors, and enum checks in the validators.

● Now the skill, so future work follows the same validation and error rules:

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 5 lines
      135  - **Commands** change state and return `Result` / `Result<TId>
           `. **Queries** read state and return `Result<TDto>`. This ligh
           tweight CQRS split lets queries later bypass the domain model 
           (e.g. `AsNoTracking` projections or Dapper) for performance wi
           thout touching commands.
      136  - Handlers implement `ICommandHandler<TCommand, TResult>` / `I
           QueryHandler<TQuery, TResult>` and are registered in `AddAppli
           cation()`.
      137  - **Validation** of input shape (required, max length, ranges)
            uses FluentValidation inside the handler before touching the 
           domain. Business invariants stay in the domain.
      138 +  - Command/query fields that come from the client are **nulla
          +ble, and enums are `string?`**. That way a missing or invalid 
          +value reaches the validator and becomes a field-level `Validat
          +ion.Failed` error, instead of failing during JSON binding with
          + a framework message. Validate enums with `.MustBeEnumName<T, 
          +TEnum>()` (member names only, case-insensitive, never numbers)
          +, then convert with `EnumNames.Parse<TEnum>()` in the handler.
      139 +  - Put field rules shared by several commands in `TaskValidat
          +ionRules`-style extension methods so Create and Update can't d
          +rift apart.                                                   
      140 +  - Messages are plain sentences with the C# property name: `"
          +Title is required."`, `"Status must be one of: ToDo, InProgres
          +s, Done."`. `errors` keys are C# property names (`Title`), not
          + camelCase.                                                   
      141 +  - `validation.ToError()` produces `Error.Validation(...)` wi
          +th per-field `ValidationErrors`. Never join messages into one 
          +string.                                                       
      142 +- **Error codes** come from `Application/Abstractions/ErrorCod
          +es.cs`, in `Area.Reason` format (`Task.NotFound`). Add new cod
          +es there; never use inline string literals, and never rename a
          +n existing code, because clients switch on them. Feature error
          +s (`TaskErrors`) use these constants.                         
      143  - **Result pattern** for expected failures (not found, validat
           ion, conflict): return `Result.Failure(Error.NotFound(...))` i
           nstead of throwing. Exceptions are for truly exceptional situa
           tions.
      144  - **DTOs** (`TaskDto`) are what Application returns. Never ret
           urn domain entities out of Application.
      145  - **One `IUnitOfWork.SaveChangesAsync`** call per command, at 
           the end of the handler, so each use case is one transaction.

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 9 lines, removed 1 line
      218  - **Status changes go through `PUT`** (agreed spec). Don't add
            action endpoints like `/start`, `/complete`, `/reopen`. Statu
           s rules belong in the domain's `Update` method.
      219  - `DELETE` is always a **soft delete**. Document that on the a
           ction, and never hard-delete through the API.
      220  - Only add endpoints that a work item in `task.md` asks for.
      221 -- **Errors use RFC 7807 ProblemDetails** everywhere: `builder.
          -Services.AddProblemDetails()` plus a .NET 8 `IExceptionHandler
          -` (`GlobalExceptionHandler`) for unhandled exceptions, and a `
          -ResultExtensions.ToProblem()` that maps `ErrorType` → status (
          -Validation→400, NotFound→404, Conflict→409). Never return stac
          -k traces to clients.                                          
      221 +- **One error format for every response** (RFC 7807 ProblemDet
          +ails + `code` + `traceId`, content type `application/problem+j
          +son`): `type`, `title` (reason phrase), `status`, `detail`, `i
          +nstance`, `code`, `traceId`, plus `errors` for validation fail
          +ures. **`Api/Infrastructure/ApiProblems.cs` is the only place 
          +that builds error bodies.** Everything routes through it:     
      222 +  - handler `Result` failures → `result.ToProblem(this)` (Erro
          +rType → 400/404/409);                                         
      223 +  - model-binding errors → `ModelStateProblems` (registered as
          + `InvalidModelStateResponseFactory`);                         
      224 +  - exceptions → `GlobalExceptionHandler` (`DomainException` 4
          +00 with its `Code`, `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` 409, `DbUpd
          +ateException` 500 `Database.Error`, anything else 500 `Server.
          +Error`);                                                      
      225 +  - empty framework responses (404 unknown route, 405, 415) → 
          +`UseStatusCodePages()` + `CustomizeProblemDetails` → `ApiProbl
          +ems.Enrich`.                                                  
      226 +  Never construct `ProblemDetails` or call `Problem()` / `BadR
          +equest(...)` directly in a controller.                        
      227 +- 5xx bodies contain only a generic message and the `traceId`,
          + never exception text, type names, SQL, or stack traces. The f
          +ull exception is logged at Error with the same `traceId`; 4xx 
          +are logged at Warning without stack traces.                   
      228 +- Don't put `[Produces("application/json")]` on controllers. I
          +t overwrites `application/problem+json` on error responses. Su
          +ccess responses default to `application/json` anyway.         
      229 +- Every new error scenario needs an integration test in `tests
          +/TaskManagement.Api.IntegrationTests` that asserts status, `co
          +de`, and the full shape (see `AssertProblem`). Those tests run
          + against the real database (agreed): keep them read-only, and 
          +simulate write failures by replacing a handler with `Configure
          +TestServices`.                                                
      230  - Request/response contracts live in `Api/Contracts` and are s
           eparate from Application commands, so the HTTP shape can evolv
           e independently.
      231  - Lists are always paginated (cap `pageSize`, e.g. at 100). Un
           bounded lists are a common scaling problem.
      232  - Annotate actions with `[ProducesResponseType]` so Swagger do
           cuments the responses.

● Both work items are done. T4 adds input validation for every endpoint and T5
  adds one consistent error format, built from your answers. The build has no
  warnings and all 56 tests pass, including 16 new integration tests against the
  real database.

  T4: Validation
  - Rules: Title is required (blank counts as missing) and limited to 200
    characters. Status and Priority must be valid names; case doesn't matter, so
    "high" is accepted. Numbers are rejected. Page size is limited to 100.
  - Error format: every validation failure returns 400 with a message per field,
    keyed by the C# name, for example "Title": ["Title is required."] or
    "Status": ["Status must be one of: ToDo, InProgress, Done."].
  - D8 (enums are part of validation): requests now send Status and Priority as
    text. An invalid value comes back as a validation error on that field
    instead of a JSON error that exposes internal type names.

  T5: Consistent error responses
  - Same format everywhere: validation failures, not found, broken domain rules,
    conflicts, server errors, unknown routes, wrong HTTP methods and wrong
    content types all return type, title, status, detail, instance, code and
    traceId.
  - 10 error codes in Area.Reason format (D7), defined in one file,
    ErrorCodes.cs (src/TaskManagement.Application/Abstractions/ErrorCodes.cs).
    Examples are Task.NotFound, Validation.Failed and Concurrency.Conflict.
  - Server errors (500) return only a generic message and the traceId. The full
    exception goes to the log under the same traceId.

  A bug the integration tests caught: a [Produces("application/json")] attribute
  on the controller was overwriting the error content type
  application/problem+json on every error response. I removed it. Normal
  responses are still application/json.

  Behaviour changes for API clients
  - Error responses look different from before: the code is now in a separate
    code field instead of title, and validation errors are listed per field.
  - Enum values must be names such as "High". Numbers like 2 used to be accepted
    and are now rejected.
  - Swagger now shows Status and Priority as plain text fields rather than
    dropdowns of allowed values. The allowed values are listed in the request
    comments.

  About D9: as you asked, the integration tests run against the real
  TaskManagementDb. They only read from it, and the failure cases (conflicts,
  database errors, crashes) use a stand-in handler that throws, so the tests
  don't change your data.

  I also updated task.md with D7–D10 and ticked off T4/T5. The skill now
  includes these validation and error-handling rules, and its code templates
  point to the real files.

      231    Never construct `ProblemDetails` or call `Problem()` / `BadR
           equest(...)` directly in a controller.
      232  - 5xx bodies contain only a generic message and the `traceId`,
            never exception text, type names, SQL, or stack traces. The f
           ull exception is logged at Error with the same `traceId`; 4xx 
           are logged at Warning without stack traces.
      233  - Don't put `[Produces("application/json")]` on controllers. I
           t overwrites `application/problem+json` on error responses. Su
           ccess responses default to `application/json` anyway.
      234 -- Every new error scenario needs an integration test in `tests
          -/TaskManagement.Api.IntegrationTests` that asserts status, `co
          -de`, and the full shape (see `AssertProblem`). Those tests run
          - against the real database (agreed): keep them read-only, and 
          -simulate write failures by replacing a handler with `Configure
          -TestServices`.                                                
      234 +- Every new error scenario needs an integration test in `tests
          +/TaskManagement.Api.IntegrationTests` that asserts status, `co
          +de`, and the full shape (see `AssertProblem`). Those tests run
          + against the real local database (agreed). Simulate failures (
          +conflicts, DB errors, crashes) by replacing a handler with `Co
          +nfigureTestServices` instead of breaking the database.        
      235 +- Integration tests that **write** (e.g. `DatabaseRoundTripTes
          +ts`) go through `TestDatabase`: every row gets the per-test `M
          +arker` in its title, assertions only look at the test's own ro
          +ws (other data may exist), and `Dispose` deletes them, so the 
          +table is left as it was. Match the marker with `LEFT(Title, LE
          +N(@Marker)) = @Marker`, not `LIKE`, because `[` is a LIKE wild
          +card. Send `DateTime` parameters as `SqlDbType.DateTime2`, bec
          +ause `AddWithValue` infers legacy `datetime` and loses precisi
          +on.                                                           
      236 +- When a feature adds or changes stored fields, add a round-tr
          +ip test proving the API returns exactly what is in SQL and sto
          +res exactly what it is sent.                                  
      237  - Request/response contracts live in `Api/Contracts` and are s
           eparate from Application commands, so the HTTP shape can evolv
           e independently.
      238  - Lists are always paginated (cap `pageSize`, e.g. at 100). Un
           bounded lists are a common scaling problem.
      239  - Annotate actions with `[ProducesResponseType]` so Swagger do
           cuments the responses.

● I've added T7 to task.md with two parts. I haven't changed any code yet.

  T7a: Sample data (at least 25 tasks)
  - What it adds: a seed script, database/seed/SeedSampleTasks.sql, with 30
    realistic tasks: 27 active and 3 soft-deleted. The deleted ones prove that
    the summary leaves them out.
  - What the data covers: every Status × Priority combination (all 12), a mix of
    assigned and unassigned tasks, some due dates including overdue ones, and
    dates that agree with each status. For example, every Done task has
    CompletedAtUtc set.
  - Safe to re-run: the tasks use fixed IDs in a recognisable range, so running
    the script twice adds nothing and never touches tasks you created yourself.
  - Also included: a script that removes only the sample rows, and a README with
    the sqlcmd command to run it.

  T7b: Raw SQL summary on GET /api/tasks/summary
  - The query: one T-SQL SELECT Status, Priority, COUNT(*) … GROUP BY Status,
    Priority, run through plain ADO.NET rather than EF Core. It filters out
    deleted tasks itself (IsDeleted = 0), because EF's automatic soft-delete
    filter doesn't apply to raw SQL.
  - Where the SQL lives: in a .sql file inside the Infrastructure project, so
    you can also run it as-is in SSMS.
  - The response: all 12 combinations with zeros filled in, plus totals per
    status, per priority and overall. The totals are added up from the same
    single query.
  - Tests: the endpoint's numbers must match the same SQL run directly against
    the database. The tests work whether or not the sample data has been loaded.

  This replaces the summary placeholder, so I marked T3 as superseded and
  updated D5.

  Decisions to confirm before I start:
  - D11, seed as a script or a migration: I recommend a T-SQL script that you
    run when you want it. An EF migration runs automatically in every
    environment, which would put sample data into production too. If you'd
    rather seed automatically everywhere, say so and I'll do it as a migration.
  - D12, summary contents: the grouped counts plus the totals described above.
  - D13, how to run the SQL: plain ADO.NET, which needs no new package. Dapper
    is the alternative.
  - D14, automatic seeding: don't load sample data when the API starts; you run
    the script yourself.

● Here's how I'm reading your answers:

  - D11: sample data goes into every environment, so it's delivered through an
    EF migration.
  - D12: the summary returns the grouped counts plus the totals, as proposed.
  - D13: the seed is a .sql file of INSERT statements that a migration runs.
    Your D13 answer was about the seed, so the original D13 question (plain
    ADO.NET or Dapper for the summary query) wasn't answered. I'll use plain
    ADO.NET, which needs no new package.
  - D14: the seed is applied as part of creating the database. It's a new
    migration that runs straight after the table migrations: on a fresh database
    all three run together in one database update, and the API's startup
    migration applies it too.

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 10 lines
      187  - Name migrations after the change: `AddTaskPriority`, `Create
           ProjectsTable`, not `Update1`.
      188  - Always read the generated migration before applying it. Watc
           h for unintended column drops/renames (EF sometimes emits drop
           +add instead of a rename, which loses data).
      189  - Never edit a migration that has already been applied to a sh
           ared database; add a new one instead.
      190 +                                                              
      191 +### Seed data (agreed in task.md T7: every environment, via mi
          +gration)                                                      
      192 +                                                              
      193 +Sample/reference data is delivered by **migrations that run `.
          +sql` files of INSERT statements**, so it is created together w
          +ith the tables in every environment (`database update`, API st
          +artup `MigrateAsync`, and `migrations script` for production).
      194 +- Put the SQL in `src/TaskManagement.Infrastructure/Persistenc
          +e/Migrations/Sql/<Name>.sql` (embedded via `<EmbeddedResource 
          +Include="Persistence\**\*.sql" />`). Create an empty migration
          + with `dotnet ef migrations add <Name>` and call `migrationBui
          +lder.Sql(SqlScripts.Read("<Name>.sql"))` in `Up()`. `Down()` r
          +uns a matching `Remove<Name>.sql`.                            
      195 +- Seed rows use **fixed Ids in a recognisable range** (current
          + seed: `5EED0000-0000-0000-0000-0000000000NN`) and `WHERE NOT 
          +EXISTS`, so the script is idempotent and `Down()` removes only
          + seed rows, never user data.                                  
      196 +- Seed values must obey the same rules as the API: valid enum 
          +names, max lengths, and consistent audit fields (`CompletedAtU
          +tc` only for Done, `ModifiedDate` for touched rows, `DeletedDa
          +te` only for deleted rows). Make dates relative to `SYSUTCDATE
          +TIME()` so the data always looks current.                     
      197 +- No `GO` and no `BEGIN TRAN` in seed files: EF runs each migr
          +ation as a single batch inside its own transaction.           
      198 +- To change seed data later, add a **new** migration (e.g. `Up
          +dateSampleTasks`) instead of editing `SeedSampleTasks.sql`, be
          +cause the old file has already run in other environments.     
      199 +- `SeedDataTests` asserts the seed exists. Integration tests m
          +ust still never depend on exact seed counts for other assertio
          +ns; they use their own marker rows or before/after deltas.    
      200  - In Development, the app may auto-apply migrations at startup
            (`db.Database.MigrateAsync()` guarded by `IsDevelopment()`). 
           For production, generate an idempotent script (`dotnet ef migr
           ations script --idempotent`) or a migration bundle. Don't auto
           -migrate in production.
      201  
      202  ### Connection strings

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 2 lines, removed 1 line
      317  These are not needed on day one. Keep them in mind so early de
           cisions don't block them:
      318  
      319  - **Bounded contexts / modular monolith:** when a second domai
           n area appears (e.g. Identity, Notifications), give it its own
            `Domain/Application/Infrastructure` set (or folder module) wi
           th its own `DbContext` and schema (`modelBuilder.HasDefaultSch
           ema("tasks")`). Contexts communicate through integration event
           s or application interfaces, never by sharing entities.
      320 -- **Read side:** heavy list/report endpoints can use dedicated
          - query handlers with `AsNoTracking()` projections straight to 
          -DTOs, or Dapper, without loading aggregates.                  
      320 +- **Read side:** heavy list/report endpoints can use dedicated
          + query handlers with `AsNoTracking()` projections straight to 
          +DTOs, or raw SQL, without loading aggregates.                 
      321 +- **Raw SQL (no EF) pattern**, as used by `GET /api/tasks/summ
          +ary`: define the interface in Application (`ITaskSummaryQuery`
          +). Implement it in `Infrastructure/Persistence/Queries/` with 
          +**plain ADO.NET** (`SqlConnectionFactory` → `SqlCommand`, time
          +out, `CancellationToken`; no Dapper). Keep the SQL in an embed
          +ded `Persistence/Queries/Sql/<Name>.sql`, read via `SqlScripts
          +.Read`. Raw SQL **bypasses the EF soft-delete query filter**, 
          +so every query must say `WHERE IsDeleted = 0` itself. Use para
          +meters for every input, never string concatenation. `SqlExcept
          +ion` maps to 500 `Database.Error`.                            
      322  - **Cross-cutting concerns** (logging, validation, transaction
           s) can later move into handler decorators.
      323  - **Outbox pattern:** if domain events must reliably trigger e
           xternal side effects (emails, message bus), persist them to an
            outbox table in the same transaction and publish them in a ba
           ckground worker.
      324  - **Auth:** add JWT bearer authentication in Api and `[Authori
           ze]` on controllers. Put the current user behind an `ICurrentU
           ser` interface in Application.

● Update(.claude\skills\task-mgmt-api\SKILL.md)
  ⎿  Added 1 line
      242  - 5xx bodies contain only a generic message and the `traceId`,
            never exception text, type names, SQL, or stack traces. The f
           ull exception is logged at Error with the same `traceId`; 4xx 
           are logged at Warning without stack traces.
      243  - Don't put `[Produces("application/json")]` on controllers. I
           t overwrites `application/problem+json` on error responses. Su
           ccess responses default to `application/json` anyway.
      244  - Every new error scenario needs an integration test in `tests
           /TaskManagement.Api.IntegrationTests` that asserts status, `co
           de`, and the full shape (see `AssertProblem`). Those tests run
            against the real local database (agreed). Simulate failures (
           conflicts, DB errors, crashes) by replacing a handler with `Co
           nfigureTestServices` instead of breaking the database.
      245 +- Test classes that write to or count rows in the database car
          +ry `[Collection(DatabaseCollection.Name)]` so they never run i
          +n parallel with each other.                                   
      246  - Integration tests that **write** (e.g. `DatabaseRoundTripTes
           ts`) go through `TestDatabase`: every row gets the per-test `M
           arker` in its title, assertions only look at the test's own ro
           ws (other data may exist), and `Dispose` deletes them, so the 
           table is left as it was. Match the marker with `LEFT(Title, LE
           N(@Marker)) = @Marker`, not `LIKE`, because `[` is a LIKE wild
           card. Send `DateTime` parameters as `SqlDbType.DateTime2`, bec
           ause `AddWithValue` infers legacy `datetime` and loses precisi
           on.
      247  - When a feature adds or changes stored fields, add a round-tr
           ip test proving the API returns exactly what is in SQL and sto
           res exactly what it is sent.
      248  - Request/response contracts live in `Api/Contracts` and are s
           eparate from Application commands, so the HTTP shape can evolv
           e independently.

● T7 is done. The database now holds 30 sample tasks, loaded by an EF migration
  that runs a SQL file of INSERT statements. GET /api/tasks/summary returns
  counts grouped by status and priority from one raw SQL query that doesn't use
  EF Core. The build has no warnings and all 72 tests pass.

  Sample data (D11, D13, D14)
  - The INSERT file: SeedSampleTasks.sql (src/TaskManagement.Infrastructure/Pers
    istence/Migrations/Sql/SeedSampleTasks.sql) has 30 realistic tasks: 27
    active and 3 soft-deleted. The active ones cover all 12 status/priority
    combinations.
  - How it runs: the new migration SeedSampleTasks executes that file right
    after the table migrations. That means every environment gets the sample
    data, whether it's updated with dotnet ef database update, when the API
    starts in Development, or through a migration script generated for
    production. I checked that the seed is in that script.
  - Fresh database: I tested on a throwaway database. One update created the
    database, ran both table migrations and inserted the 30 rows. I dropped it
    afterwards.
  - Safe to undo: the sample tasks have fixed IDs (5EED0000-…-0000000000NN).
    Rolling the migration back removed exactly those 30 rows and kept a task I
    had added myself, and re-applying it restored them.
  - Dates: they're calculated when the migration runs, so the data always looks
    recent. A few tasks are overdue on purpose.

  Summary endpoint (D12)
  - The query: it's in TaskSummary.sql
    (src/TaskManagement.Infrastructure/Persistence/Queries/Sql/TaskSummary.sql),
    so you can also run it as-is in SSMS: SELECT Status, Priority, COUNT(*) …
    WHERE IsDeleted = 0 GROUP BY Status, Priority. The API runs it through plain
    ADO.NET with your Windows-authentication connection.
  - Deleted tasks: the query excludes them itself, because EF's automatic
    soft-delete filter doesn't apply to raw SQL.
  - Live response with the sample data: total 27, the 12 grouped counts (zeros
    would be included), byStatus ToDo 10 / InProgress 9 / Done 8, and byPriority
    Low 7 / Medium 8 / High 7 / Critical 5. This matches running the SQL file
    directly.
  - Database errors: a failure in this query now returns the standard 500
    Database.Error response.

  Tests: 11 new ones.
  - 3 unit tests check that missing combinations come back as zeros and that the
    totals add up.
  - 4 summary tests check that the API matches a separate GROUP BY run directly
    on SQL Server, that adding tasks changes the counts by exactly that number,
    and that deleted tasks are ignored.
  - 4 seed tests check the 30 rows, all 12 combinations, and that the dates
    agree with each status.

  Tests that change or count database rows now run one at a time so they can't
  affect each other's numbers.

  About D13: your answer described the seed file, so the original question
  (plain ADO.NET or Dapper for the summary query) was never answered. I used
  plain ADO.NET, which needed no new package.

  Changing the sample data later: add a new migration rather than editing
  SeedSampleTasks.sql. That file has already run, and editing it wouldn't change
  any database where it was applied.

  task.md has T7 ticked with your decisions, and T2f/T3 are closed, so no open
  items are left. The skill now includes the rules for seeding through
  migrations and for raw SQL queries.

● I've added two work items to task.md, T8 and T9, and listed the new login
  endpoint in the API section's endpoint table. I haven't changed any code.

  T8: Authentication API
  - The endpoint: POST /api/auth/login takes { username, password } from the UI
    and returns a Bearer access token with its expiry time.
  - Dummy user: abc123@yopmail.com / abc123, recorded in the task as temporary
    and for development and testing only.
  - Every outcome is listed: success, a username in different capitals, missing
    or invalid fields (400), wrong credentials (401) and too many attempts
    (429).
  - Wrong credentials: an unknown username and a wrong password get the
    identical response, so the API never reveals which usernames exist.
  - Task APIs protected: every /api/tasks call needs the token; without it you
    get 401 in the standard error format. New error codes:
    Auth.InvalidCredentials, Auth.Unauthorized, Auth.TooManyAttempts.
  - Security rules:
    - The password abc123 is never stored as plain text in code or config.
      Config holds only a password hash, and the dummy user exists only in the
      Development settings, so this well-known login can't work on a deployed
      server.
    - The token signing key goes in user-secrets or an environment variable,
      never in the repository.
    - Passwords are never logged.
    - Login attempts are rate-limited.
    - CORS allows only the UI's address.

  T9: Tests for every API, success and failure
  - What exists today: 72 tests. I listed the gaps: no handler tests for
    get-by-id and list, no paging tests, and no tests yet for a PUT on a deleted
    task, field-length limits through the API, or anything to do with
    authentication.
  - A test matrix for each endpoint (login, list, get, create, update, delete,
    summary) with success and failure columns. Cases already covered are ticked
    ✅ so only the missing ones remain.
  - Login protection tests for all 6 task endpoints: no token, a malformed
    token, an expired token, a wrong signing key, and the wrong issuer or
    audience.
  - Supporting work: a helper that logs the test client in, updating existing
    tests to send the token once login is required, and a code coverage report.

  Decisions to confirm before I start:
  - D15: all task APIs require login, and only login itself is open. I recommend
    yes.
  - D16: the dummy user lives in Development config, stored as a hash. A Users
    table with a seed migration would put this known password into every
    environment, because your D11 decision sends seeds everywhere. So I don't
    recommend that for credentials.
  - D17: the token is a JWT valid for 60 minutes, with no refresh token for now.
  - D18: the username is always an email address.
  - D19: 5 login attempts per minute per client, then 429.
  - D20: I need your input. What address does the UI run on, for example
    http://localhost:4200? CORS needs it so the UI can call the API.
  - D21: coverage targets of Domain ≥ 95%, Application ≥ 90% and overall ≥ 85%.

> D15: All api will be using the access token generated by authentication,
  which will be use to authorize to run the api. Login will happen only once
  and the asme token will be used for all the api
  D16: dont include the username and password . it is what we will be using dor
  testing purpose
  D17: Yes the token is jwt and its valid for 60 minutes, no refresh token as
  of now
  D18: yes the username will be always email address
  D19: User can make any login attempts no restriction

      28  **This project targets .NET 8 only. That is a fixed project dec
          ision.** Every project uses `net8.0`. Every `Microsoft.*` / EF 
          Core package uses an **8.0.x** version, and the `dotnet-ef` too
          l is 8.0.x. Don't upgrade to .NET 9/10 or EF Core 9/10, and don
          't accept a package version that needs a newer framework, even 
          if a newer SDK is installed or a tool suggests it. When adding 
          a package with `dotnet add package`, always pass `--version` ex
          plicitly. Otherwise NuGet picks the latest major version, which
           targets a newer framework and fails restore or silently drifts
          . Third-party packages (FluentValidation, Swashbuckle, NetArchT
          est, xUnit) must use versions that support `net8.0`.

      331  - **Raw SQL (no EF) pattern**, as used by `GET /api/tasks/summ
           ary`: define the interface in Application (`ITaskSummaryQuery`
           ). Implement it in `Infrastructure/Persistence/Queries/` with 
           **plain ADO.NET** (`SqlConnectionFactory` → `SqlCommand`, time
           out, `CancellationToken`; no Dapper). Keep the SQL in an embed
           ded `Persistence/Queries/Sql/<Name>.sql`, read via `SqlScripts
           .Read`. Raw SQL **bypasses the EF soft-delete query filter**, 
           so every query must say `WHERE IsDeleted = 0` itself. Use para
           meters for every input, never string concatenation. `SqlExcept
           ion` maps to 500 `Database.Error`.
      332  - **Cross-cutting concerns** (logging, validation, transaction
           s) can later move into handler decorators.
      333  - **Outbox pattern:** if domain events must reliably trigger e
           xternal side effects (emails, message bus), persist them to an
            outbox table in the same transaction and publish them in a ba
           ckground worker.
      334 -- **Auth:** add JWT bearer authentication in Api and `[Authori
          -ze]` on controllers. Put the current user behind an `ICurrentU
          -ser` interface in Application.                                
      334 +- **Auth (done, see §6a):** if handlers later need the logged-
          +in user, put it behind an `ICurrentUser` interface in Applicat

      257  - Lists are always paginated (cap `pageSize`, e.g. at 100). Un
           bounded lists are a common scaling problem.
      258  - Annotate actions with `[ProducesResponseType]` so Swagger do

      260 +### 6a. Authentication and authorization (task.md T8, D15–D20)
      261 +                                                              
      262 +- **Every endpoint requires a JWT** through the authorization 
          +**fallback policy** (`AuthSetup.AddApiAuth`). New controllers 
          +are protected automatically; don't add `[Authorize]` everywher
          +e, and **only** mark an action `[AllowAnonymous]` when the use
          +r explicitly asks (today only `POST /api/auth/login`). Clients
          + log in once and reuse the token for 60 minutes; there is no r
          +efresh token.                                                 
      263 +- Login flow: `AuthController` → `LoginCommand` (validator: em
          +ail ≤ 256, password ≤ 128) → `IUserAuthenticator` → `IAccessTo
          +kenService`. Unknown user and wrong password return the **iden
          +tical** 401 `Auth.InvalidCredentials`.                        
      264 +- **Credentials and secrets never go in the repository**: no u
          +sernames/passwords in code, config, docs, `.http` files, or te
          +sts (D16). Test users live in **dotnet user-secrets** (`script
          +s/Set-DevAuthUser.ps1 -Username … -Password …` stores only an 
          +Identity V3 password hash) and only work in Development (`Auth
          +Options.Users` is cleared in other environments). The JWT sign
          +ing key comes from user-secrets or the `Jwt__SigningKey` envir
          +onment variable; startup fails without it. Never log, echo, or
          + return a password (records redact it in `ToString()`).       
      265 +- Token validation: issuer `TaskManagement.Api`, audience `Tas
          +kManagement.Ui`, lifetime with 1 minute skew, HS256 only, `Map
          +InboundClaims = false` (claims `sub`, `email`, `jti`). Issuing
          + and validating share `JwtSigningKey.Create`.                 
      266 +- 401/403 from the framework go through `UseStatusCodePages` →
          + `ApiProblems` (`Auth.Unauthorized` / `Auth.Forbidden`). Anony
          +mous requests to unknown routes also get 401 (by design, becau
          +se of the fallback policy).                                   
      267 +- CORS: only `Cors:AllowedOrigins` (currently `https://localho
          +st:5173`, the React dev server); methods GET/POST/PUT/DELETE; 
          +headers `Authorization`, `Content-Type`.                      
      268 +- Application stays free of auth technology: no JWT, Identity,
          + or ASP.NET types (the architecture test enforces this).      
      269 +

● T8 (login) and T9 (tests) are both done. All 312 tests pass in NUnit, the
  build has 0 warnings, and unit tests cover 100% of the Domain and Application
  code, above your 90% target.

  Login and protected APIs (D15–D20)
  - Login: POST /api/auth/login takes an email and password and returns a JWT
    that's valid for 60 minutes. The UI logs in once and sends that token on
    every call. There's no refresh token and no limit on login attempts.
  - Every other API needs the token. Without a valid one it returns 401 in the
    standard error format with code Auth.Unauthorized. Endpoints added later are
    protected automatically.
  - Wrong credentials: a wrong password and an unknown user get exactly the same
    401 response, so the API never reveals which emails exist.
  - CORS: only https://localhost:5173 can call the API from a browser. When the
    React app has its real address, change Cors:AllowedOrigins in
    appsettings.json.
  - Swagger has an "Authorize" button where you can paste the token.
  - Checked live: your test login works, the token works on every task API, a
    missing or tampered token gets 401, and other browser origins are blocked.

  D16: the test username and password are not in any file in the repo.
  - I put them in user-secrets on this machine, which live outside the repo.
    Only the password hash is stored, together with a random signing key for the
    tokens.
  - Another developer sets theirs up once with ./scripts/Set-DevAuthUser.ps1
    -Username <email> -Password <password>.
  - I also removed the credentials I had written into task.md earlier, and a
    scan finds no trace of them anywhere in the repo.
  - These test logins only work in Development; on any other environment they're
    switched off.

  Tests (D21)
  - All 5 test projects moved from xUnit to NUnit, plus a new project for the
    Infrastructure code (password checking, tokens, SQL scripts):

  │ Integration (real database) │ 146   │

  - What the integration tests cover: every login case; every task endpoint with
    eight kinds of missing or bad token; CORS; and create, read, update, delete
    and paging. They use their own throwaway user and a random signing key, so
    they need no real credentials. They leave the database with just the 30
    sample tasks.
  - Coverage: unit tests alone cover 100% of Domain and Application lines. All
    tests together cover 97.3% of the production code.
  - Specs.md is new and has the NUnit notes: conventions, the rules for database
    tests, the success/failure checklist per endpoint, and how to produce the
    coverage report.

  Things you should know
  - NUnit is pinned to 4.4.0. Newer 4.6 versions don't compile with the C#
    version in the .NET 8 SDK, so don't upgrade it on its own.
  - Requests without a token to unknown URLs get 401, not 404. That follows from
    "every endpoint needs the token" and doesn't reveal which URLs exist. With
    a token you get the normal 404 or 405.
  - Bug fixed: an empty status or priority filter was rejected by validation
    even though the handler treats it as "no filter". Both now treat it as no
    filter.
  - Coverage setting fixed: my first coverage settings left every async method
    out of the measurement, which made the number look better than it was. The
    figures above include all the code.
  - Still untested: a few error-handling branches, such as a client cancelling a
    request mid-way. They're listed as the one open item in task.md.

  I've updated task.md with D15–D21 and the results, and the skill now includes
  the authentication rules, the "no credentials in the repo" rule and the NUnit
  requirement.
